It all just sort of caught up with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself acting like this in other situations before?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, not that I can think of. I mean, I've always liked being involved in my community groups, but... I don't think I've ever gone quite this overboard before. Usually I know my limits and just stick to my normal routine. So this whole thing was pretty unusual for me.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were able to stop yourself from taking on more and more responsibilities with the fundraiser, or did it feel like it was hard to control?\n\nPatient: It felt really hard to control. I just couldn't seem to stop myself. Every time someone brought up a new idea or a problem, I just jumped right in and said I'd handle it. It was like I was on autopilot, just saying yes to everything, even when a part of me knew I was already in over my head. I didn't even think about slowing down until it all crashed down on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had issues with impulsivity in other areas of your life, such as spending money or impulsive decisions?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I'm usually pretty careful with my money, and I don't really make big decisions on the fly. Like I said, I usually just stick to my normal routine. That's why this whole thing with the fundraiser was so strange for me.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any feelings of euphoria or excitement when you were taking on all these responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. At first, I felt... well, almost glowing. It was like every idea I had was just wonderful. But, um, if anyone questioned me or my plans, I got really snappish. I just acted like they were standing in the way.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were somehow special or uniquely qualified to handle all these responsibilities with the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I, um... I truly believed my heritage quilt ideas were going to put our little town on the map. It just felt like I had finally found the thing that only I could make happen. I really thought I was the only person who could see the whole vision and do it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling sad, empty, or depressed over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I really haven't felt down or sad at all lately. If anything, it's just been... well, all that energy and focus I had with the fundraiser. So, no, I haven't had any periods of feeling empty or depressed.\n\nDoctor: Was this feeling of being almost glowing and having wonderful ideas constant while you were working on the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not really sure if it was constant every single minute, but... yeah, it pretty much lasted the whole time. I didn't really have any breaks from feeling that way until the fundraiser was over.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever have a day where you didn't feel that glowing or excited about the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I can't think of a single day where I woke up and didn't feel that excitement. Like I was saying, it just stayed with me the whole time. There wasn't really a day where it stopped or faded until the whole thing was finally over.\n\nDoctor: Did your excited and glowing feeling ever vary in intensity from one moment to another while you were working on the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I didn't really notice if it went up or down from minute to minute. It just felt like a steady level of energy to me. I was so busy getting things done that I wasn't really paying attention to the exact intensity of it, you know? It just felt strong the whole time.\n\nDoctor: Did your excited and glowing feeling with the fundraiser ever go away for a little while and then come back?\n\nPatient: No, it didn't really go away and come back. It was just... there. The whole time I was working on it, that feeling stayed with me. It didn't take any breaks.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel this impulsivity in other areas of your life during the same time period as the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I was just so focused on the fundraiser that I wasn't really doing much else. Everything was going into that one project, so I didn't really have the time or the chance to be impulsive with anything else, you know? It was all just about getting the event put together.\n\nDoctor: Did you act impulsively in any other areas of your life during the time you were working on the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I really didn't. I was just so completely wrapped up in the fundraiser. I didn't have the time or energy to do anything else, impulsive or not. Everything I had was going straight into that project.\n\nDoctor: Was your impulsivity during that time limited to just the fundraiser, or did it affect other parts of your life as well?\n\nPatient: I feel like I'm repeating myself a bit here, but yes, it was strictly limited to the fundraiser. Like I said, I didn't really have anything else going on right then. My whole focus was just on that one event, so it didn't affect any other parts of my life.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any periods of significantly decreased need for sleep during the time you were working on the fundraiser?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I was sleeping about the same as usual, maybe a little less just because I was busy, but I wasn't staying up all night or anything like that. I still needed my rest.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
